hallway or through the air vent saying things like, “You’re going to lose it all,” and it sounds so real that I’ve gone outside to see who was there.\n- key: S022\n  name: There are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has manifestation: My sister will tell me something funny or upsetting and I know I should react, but my face feels blank and it takes effort to answer with more than a few words.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to plan brunches, paint in the spare room, go hiking, and sign up for networking events, but now those things feel like tasks I’m pretending to care about.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or pushing through wet cement.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach Um… more slowed down, I think. Not really restless.

manifestation: My sister will tell me something funny or upsetting and I know I should react, but my face feels blank and it takes effort to answer with more than a few words.\n- key: S025\n  name: I’m not sure if I’m answering exactly what you’re asking, but it hasn’t really felt like it fully let up.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you generally had low self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I try not to think of it as “low self-esteem,” exactly, but I do feel like when things go wrong it must be because I messed up. Like I’m not doing well enough at work, or I’m failing at taking care of my home… and then it turns into this feeling that maybe I deserve to lose the things I worked for. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t always call it hopeless, because I can still, like, get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do. But I came in because I can’t seem to stop thinking that when something goes wrong, it’s my fault… like I’m just a bad employee, or I’m not handling being a homeowner the way I should.\n\nAnd then it kind of turns into this feeling that maybe I deserve to lose the things I worked for. I know that sounds harsh, sorry… but that’s the thought I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot of different things,” exactly. I mean, I do worry, but it’s usually about whether I’m doing things right or messing something up. I’m not sure if that counts as excessive… sorry, I’m trying to answer it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings mainly come up when you think about your townhouse and the responsibilities that came with it?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… the townhouse is a big part of it, because it feels like proof that I’m either handling my life or I’m not. It spills over into work too, like if anything goes wrong I immediately think it’s because I failed somehow… like I’m a bad employee, or I shouldn’t have taken on being a homeowner.\n\nI know that’s probably not the most balanced way to see it, but I keep getting stuck on this thought that I deserve to lose what I worked for. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’ve just been feeling really down on myself. Like, I know things go wrong for everyone, but I keep feeling like it’s all somehow my fault. I just haven’t been able to shake it.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after a specific stressful event or major change in your life?\n\nPatient: I think it kind of started after I bought my townhouse. I mean, there were little stretches where I could function a bit better and maybe not feel it as strongly, but I don’t think I ever had more than two months where it was actually gone. I’m not sure if I’m answering exactly what you’re asking, but it hasn’t really felt like it fully let up.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you generally had low self-esteem?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I try not to think of it as “low self-esteem,” exactly, but I do feel like when things go wrong it must be because I messed up. Like I’m not doing well enough at work, or I’m failing at taking care of my home… and then it turns into this feeling that maybe I deserve to lose the things I worked for. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you often felt hopeless?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t always call it hopeless, because I can still, like, get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do. But I came in because I can’t seem to stop thinking that when something goes wrong, it’s my fault… like I’m just a bad employee, or I’m not handling being a homeowner the way I should.\n\nAnd then it kind of turns into this feeling that maybe I deserve to lose the things I worked for. I know that sounds harsh, sorry… but that’s the thought I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “a lot of different things,” exactly. I mean, I do worry, but it’s usually about whether I’m doing things right or messing something up. I’m not sure if that counts as excessive… sorry, I’m trying to answer it the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings mainly come up when you think about your townhouse and the responsibilities that came with it?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… the townhouse is a big part of it, because it feels like proof that I’m either handling my life or I’m not. But it’s not only that. It spills over into work too, like if anything goes wrong I immediately think it’s because I failed somehow… like I’m a bad employee, or I shouldn’t have taken on being a homeowner.\n\nI know that’s probably not the most balanced way to see it, but I keep getting stuck on this thought that I deserve to lose what I worked for. Is that… is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: If the stress around the townhouse were taken away, do you think these feelings would mostly go away?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes, because that would make it simpler. The townhouse definitely makes it louder, if that makes sense. But I don’t think it would mostly go away.\n\nEven when it’s work stuff, or just anything that doesn’t go right, I go straight to thinking it’s my fault… like I’m not doing enough, or I’m bad at the things I’m supposed to be responsible for. And then I get this really ugly thought that maybe I deserve to lose what I worked for.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic. I’m sorry. But no, I don’t think taking away just the townhouse stress would fix it completely.\n\nDoctor: During this period, was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when your depression was clearly worse than your usual baseline?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, it’s hard for me to separate out what’s “baseline” anymore, because the depression and not really enjoying things have kind of been there for a long time now—about two years and a month, I’d say.\n\nSo… yes, there have been stretches where it felt clearly worse, but honestly the low mood itself has been pretty constant for that whole time. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: During one of those times when your depression felt clearly worse than usual, did you have thoughts that you would be better off dead or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure how to answer that cleanly. I don’t think I want to hurt myself, if that’s what you mean. I haven’t really thought of it as a plan or anything like that.\n\nThere have been moments where I’ve felt really tired of myself, or like people might be better off not having to deal with me, but I don’t know if that counts as “better off dead.” I’m sorry, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: During one of those times when your depression felt clearly worse than usual, did it last at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… it did. I mean, I think it’s been more than just a two-week thing, honestly. The depression and not really having interest in things has been there for about two years and a month.\n\nThere are worse stretches inside that, but the overall feeling has lasted a lot longer than two weeks. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: During one of those clearly worse periods, did your sleep change noticeably?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I think it did. I mean, I still got some sleep, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most nights I’d be lying there for hours just listening to the house settle.\n\nAnd then I’d wake up around three or four in the morning and just… not really be able to get back to sleep before work. Is that the kind of sleep change you mean?\n\nDoctor: During one of those worse periods, did your appetite change noticeably?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it did. I don’t always notice it right away, because it feels like I’m just busy or not thinking about food, but during those worse times I’ll skip actual meals during the day.\n\nIt’s kind of embarrassing, but I’ll end up mostly having coffee, maybe toast, and then whatever is easiest later. And then I’ll notice my clothes feel looser and realize I haven’t really been eating normally. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: During one of those worse periods, did you feel slowed down in your body or movements, or so restless that you couldn’t sit still?\n\nPatient: Um… more slowed down, I think. Not really restless.\n\nI try not to make a big thing of it, because I still get myself to work, but some mornings everything feels like it takes so much effort. Like opening spreadsheets, typing out replies, even walking to a meeting… it feels like I’m pushing through wet cement.\n\nI don’t know if other people notice it, or if I just feel it internally, but it’s definitely there during the worse periods.\n\nDoctor: During one of those worse periods, did you have trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I have days where I’m not as sharp, but I don’t know if it’s enough to call it “trouble concentrating.”\n\nAt work I can usually still get through things if I make lists or reread emails a couple times. It’s more like I feel foggy, maybe, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I can still do what I need to do, mostly. So… maybe some low energy, but I’m not sure if it’s the kind you mean.\n\nDoctor: During one of those worse periods, did you feel depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. But no, I don’t think taking away just the townhouse stress would fix it completely.\n\nDoctor: During this period, was there ever a stretch of at least two weeks when your depression was clearly worse than your usual baseline?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so.
